FINANCIAL AND HUMAN RESOURCES\n\n        Obligations\n\n        1.   \"Each Contracting Party shall take the appropriate steps to ensure that adequate\n             financial resources are available to support the safety of each nuclear installation\n             throughout its lifetime.”\n\n        2. “Each Contracting Party shall take the appropriate steps to ensure that sufficient\n           numbers of qualified staff with appropriate education, training and retraining,\n           are available for all safety-related activities in or for each nuclear installation\n           throughout its lifetime.”\n\nArticle 11 (1)        Financial Resources\n\n   -    Mechanisms for the provision of financial resources to the licence holder\n        or applicant in order to ensure safety of the nuclear installation throughout\n        its lifetime, including\n\n       Principles for the financing of safety improvements to the nuclear\n        installation over its operational lifetime\n\n        Financial resources to assure a safe and reliable operation and maintenance of\n        Laguna Verde Nuclear Power Station (LVNPS) come from the Federal Government.\n        They are requested via the application for authorisation of the budget as part of the\n        strategic and operative programmes.\n\n        The Operation and Maintenance expenditures are privileged at LVNPS as compared\n        to the rest of CFE facilities; since the requirements of the Mexican Regulatory Body\n        must be met to maintain compliance with the Authorisation for Operation of LVNPS\n        Units.\n\n        The management process of the financial resources contributes to meeting the plant\n        safety standards during normal operation, refuelling outages and major maintenance\n        programmes.\n\n       Principles for financial provisions during the period of commercial\n        operation for decommissioning and management of spent fuel and\n        radioactive waste from nuclear installations\n\n        The costs for decommissioning, and handling and disposal of radioactive waste from\n        LVNPS at the end of its lifetime are booked and appear in the financial statements.\n        This provision is calculated based on the legal requirement jointly issued by LVNPS\n        and CFE’s corporate accountant, where it indicates that LVNPS will integrate a fund\n        of 1,206.13 million dollars US (in 2015) for both units, in accordance with the\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                            11-1\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                 NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        methodology established by the USNRC in 10 CFR Part 50.75, with data from NUREG\n        1307 Rev. 15. This amount will be periodically updated based on data issued in new\n        revisions of NUREG 1307. This fund will be integrated into the production costs for\n        future storage of radioactive waste and decommissioning.\n\n        LVNPS has chosen a method of decommissioning is named DECON, in accordance\n        with the provisions of NUREG/CR-6174. The method has four stages of work:\n\n             1. Pre-Decommissioning Engineering and Planning\n\n                 Before the cease of the activity, it is the stage of planning, engineering and\n                 review of the regulation.\n\n             2. Reactor Deactivation for the Safe Storage\n\n                 The cease of activity and preparation of the installation for storage is carried out.\n\n             3. Safe Storage and Spent Fuel Management\n\n                 Period of safe storage in the plant and operations in the spent fuel pool until its\n                 inventory of fuel is zero.\n\n             4. Dismantlement\n\n                 Decontamination and dismantling of the radioactive parts of the plant is being\n                 carried out in order to complete the nuclear reactor licensing.\n\n        As part of the extended period for safe storage of spent fuel, there is the following:\n\n        Prior to the end of useful life of LVNPS’s Independent Spent Fuel Storage Installation\n        (ISFSI), HOLTEC International, Inc.’s MultiPurpose Canisters (MPC), loaded with spent\n        fuel, will be transferred to its overpack, called HI-STORM FW, to a transport container,\n        called HI-STAR, for transportation off-site, to an approved final disposal site.\n\n        Because the MPC canisters are designed for storage, transport and disposal of spent\n        fuel, the fuel cells will remain sealed inside the canisters, so decontamination of the\n        MPC canisters is not required for transportation to the final repository.\n\n        After the transfer off-site of the MPC canisters, the ISFSI can be dismantled, identifying\n        and removing the radioactive waste, and creating a final radiological report.\n\n        CFE will evaluate and modify the existing analysis of the dismantling of LVNPS Units\n        1 and 2 to include the financing cost of dismantling the ISFSI.\n\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener                Period 2019-2021\n                                                            11-2\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n   -    Statement with regard to the adequacy of financial provisions\n\n        Financial resources for the safe and reliable operation of LVNPS are assured by the\n        Federal Government budget. Furthermore, laws take into account additional\n        resources to respond to contingencies and/or urgent expenses.\n\n        LVNPS includes within its own budget annual financial resources for recruiting,\n        qualification, training and retraining of the nuclear installation’s personnel. Through\n        that, it guarantees the knowledge transfer and compliance with nuclear-related\n        regulations.\n\n   -    Contracting Party’s processes to assess the financial provisions\n\n        According to Article 27 of the Constitution, the generation of nuclear energy and the\n        regulation of its application belong exclusively to the Mexican State, so this state is\n        responsible for annually assuring the financial resources for the safe operation of the\n        installation.\n\n   -    Description of the Contracting Party’s arrangements for ensuring that the\n        necessary financial resources are available in the event of a radiological\n        emergency\n\n        CFE’s Nuclear Corporate Coordination (NCC) has, by its nature, two special policies:\n\n                LVNPS Liability Policy\n                LVNPS Physical Damage Policy\n\n        The general conditions of these policies are as follows: Indemnities that legally or by\n        order of competent judicial authority, the Insured must pay to third parties for\n        damages to persons and/or property as a result of an event or succession of events\n        originating from a nuclear or non-nuclear accident occurring at the LVNPS and/or\n        from the use of radioactive facilities.\n\nArticle 11 (2)        Human Resources\n\n   -    Overview of the Contracting Party’s arrangements and regulatory\n        requirements concerning staffing, qualification, training and retraining of\n        staff for nuclear installations;\n\n        For staffing, qualification, training and retraining of staff for nuclear installations,\n        CNSNS and the licence holders agreed at the outset to implement the requirements\n        of standard ANSI/ANS 3.1-1981 \"Selection, Qualification and Training of Personnel for\n        Nuclear Power Plants.\" However, in the case of nuclear power plants, in November,\n        2000, the requirements of the Mexican Official Standard NOM-NUCL-034\n        \"Requirements for Selection, Qualification and Training of Personnel for Nuclear\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                            11-3\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        Power Plants” entered into force. Currently, this standard has the version NOM-034-\n        NUCL-2016 issued in December 2, 2016.\n\n       Human Resources of CFE’s Nuclear Corporate Coordination\n\n        The workforce of CFE’s Nuclear Corporate Coordination (CCN) amounts to 1400\n        permanent employees as of March 2022.\n\n       Organisation of CFE’s Nuclear Corporate Coordination\n\n        The CFE organization currently approved in the Quality Assurance Plan Rev. 13D has a\n        General Manager and 5 Directors of: Financed Investment Projects, Finance,\n        Operation, Administration, Modernization and Structural Change. Figure 11.1 shows\n        the current structure of CFE and the Nuclear Corporate Coordination.\n\n        It is worth mentioning that during the 2019-2021 period, according to the CFE's\n        organic statute of May 10, 2021, the Nuclear Corporate Coordination was authorized,\n        which is the Nuclear Generation Business Unit, and is in charge of the nuclear\n        generation assets. Likewise, it is under the immediate control of the CFE's General\n        Director and its head will be appointed by him, the above to strengthen the needs of\n        reliable and safe operation of nuclear generation assets and to meet the\n        recommendations of the IAEA, modifying the authorized structure of the Nuclear\n        Power Plants Management, to provide direction and leadership for the application of\n        independent nuclear corporate supervision, training, as well as the management of\n        operational planning and administration of human, material and financial resources\n        of the CFE's nuclear facilities.\n\n        Currently, the CNSNS is evaluating a request for a change to the Quality Assurance\n        Plan, which was submitted in September 2021. This change is related to the\n        organizational restructuring due to the creation of the Nuclear Corporate\n        Coordination and the compensated movements in order not to reduce the\n        established regulatory commitments.\n\n   -    Methods used for the analysis of competence requirements and training\n        needs for all safety related activities in nuclear installations\n\n        The Systematic Approach to Training (SAT) has been applied at LVNPS since 2006,\n        defining the training requirements for personnel performing safety-related activities.\n        This methodology is applied to the positions identified in 10 CFR 50.120; other\n        important safety-related positions are covered by training programs defined in the\n        Quality Control Plan.\n\n        The set of competencies that need to be developed and maintained by personnel for\n        a given position under SAT, is determined using the result of the Job Task Analysis,\n        which uses a list of the tasks that require training as its starting point. The selection of\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener              Period 2019-2021\n                                                            11-4\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        the method to achieve the required competencies is based on the information\n        available, which may be one or more of the following:\n\n        -     Task analysis.\n        -     Competency analysis by group of tasks.\n        -     Competency analysis based on learning objectives.\n        -     Competency analysis based on guidelines.\n\n        Additionally, to the training programs obtained from Job Task Analysis, the \"Training\n        Needs\" are collected continuously which are analysed in order to identify if changes\n        to processes and procedures, plant modifications, reworks, use of new technology,\n        actual or potential performance weaknesses, internal or external operational\n        experience, etc., require training or implementation of other actions to prevent or\n        correct performance weaknesses.\n\n        Once the issue or weakness is identified, the technical area generates a Request for\n        Training Needs Analysis. This request may also be generated after evaluating the area\n        performance or whenever the Department Head considers appropriate. Derived from\n        this Request, a meeting is called with the Area Training Committee, for their attention.\n        At that meeting, the corrective or preventive actions to be taken are defined. These\n        actions provide feedback to the training programmes in order to make them both\n        more efficient and effective.\n\n   -    Arrangements for initial training and retraining of operations staff,\n        including simulator training\n\n        The initial training for Reactor Operators (ROs) which lasts about 4 years consists of\n        basic training in LVNPS’s fundamentals and technology, standards, policies and\n        specific procedures. After about a year, the trainees are sent to the plant to become\n        familiar with all plant systems and activities. Subsequently they take a simulator\n        course in the LVNPS full-scope simulator. Once the candidate approves the simulator\n        course, he is assigned to the Main Control Room (MCR). Finally, they are prepared for\n        the licence examination by reviewing some topics, during this time they receive\n        training on the Emergency Operating Procedures (EOP), both in the classroom and\n        the simulator.\n\n        Retraining is carried out every two years and combines at least 40 hours of plant\n        simulator and 60 hours of classroom per year. The retraining covers plant systems,\n        abnormal operation and emergencies, internal and external operational experience,\n        administrative procedures, etc. Additionally, before every refuelling outage, training is\n        carried out, the content of which covers the changes and modifications to be\n        implemented, new nuclear fuel, technical specifications, and plant start-up on the\n        simulator, as well as other subjects (in accordance with national regulations).\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                            11-5\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n   -    Capabilities of plant simulators used for training with regard to fidelity to\n        the plant and scope of simulation\n\n        At the LVNPS full-scope simulator, the updates necessary have been carried out to\n        maintain physical and operational fidelity, with respect to LVNPS Unit 2.\n\n        It is important to mention Units 1 and 2 are very similar facilities, so the differences\n        between the simulator and Unit 1 are minimal. From the beginning of the training,\n        these differences are reviewed by the simulator instructors in conjunction with the\n        operating teams (licensed personnel) of Unit 1 and are included in the scenario guides.\n\n   -    Arrangements for training of maintenance and technical support staff\n\n        Training of maintenance and technical support personnel is carried out according to\n        the training plans developed under the Systematic Approach to Training and the\n        Quality Assurance Plan. These plans describe different environments for training\n        (classroom, laboratory, on-the-job training, interim assignments, etc.). Classroom\n        courses focus mainly on theory, while laboratory-based courses include theory\n        fundamentals supplemented with practical exercises in the laboratory, workshops,\n        and on mock-ups.\n\n        During the initial training, the trainees attend classroom courses or laboratory-based\n        courses. Then they are assigned to the technical area in which they are being trained.\n        The assignment duration is oriented to help the technicians “under training” acquire\n        the skills necessary to complete the qualifications as technicians. During this period,\n        the trainees keep attending training such as interim assignments or on-the-job\n        training, as well as classroom and laboratory training, or other complementary\n        training.\n\n        Later, during retraining, personnel are trained in those topics that are identified as\n        necessary for maintaining their qualifications. The topics include the actions resulting\n        from the Requests for Training Needs Analysis, operational experience, refresher\n        training or specific needs resulting from personnel performance.\n\n   -    Improvements to training programmes as a result of new insights from\n        safety analyses, operational experience, development of training methods\n        and practices, etc.\n\n        External operational experience has required improving procedures, post-drill\n        critique techniques, and the use of human performance tools. Improvements have\n        also been implemented to classroom, full-scope simulator, process simulator,\n        workshops and mock-up training.\n\n        Since 2008 a hydraulic loop or Process Simulator has been in operation for practical\n        training on technical subjects, reinforcing the adherence to plant standards, and\n        application of error prevention techniques.\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                            11-6\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        In 2013 LVNPS implemented an Internet-based system that provides workers with\n        operational experience lessons and online evaluation to reinforce the learning.\n\n        Simulator training activities and online training continue to be used in staff training\n        and qualification activities. In 2020, a simulator was implemented for the\n        commissioning of the emergency diesel generator.\n\n   -    Methods used to assess the sufficiency of staff at nuclear installations\n\n        During the initial training, personnel are evaluated on theoretical aspects through\n        written examinations, while the practical skills are evaluated on-the-job training. For\n        licenced personnel (Reactor Operator [RO] and Senior Reactor Operator [SRO]) the\n        initial training is very rigorous with multiple written examinations, while the practical\n        skills are assessed in the full-scope simulator. The job performance, for all personnel\n        executing safety-related functions, is evaluated by observers, using an evaluation\n        card. The results are used to determine a Performance Indicator to take corrective\n        actions when deviations are identified.\n\n        The evaluation of the RO and SRO licensed personnel (to obtain a license) is carried\n        out by the CNSNS. For non-licensed personnel in the different technical areas, the\n        partial evaluation is controlled by the LVNPS´s Central Training Center, but the final\n        release to perform the functions of the position is authorized by the Head of the\n        Technical Department after their field stays.\n\n   -    Policy or principles governing the use of contracted personnel to support\n        or supplement the licensee’s own staff\n\n        The principles governing the use of contractor personnel to support or supplement\n        LVNPS staff are established considering two types of contractors:\n\n        1.    Contractors who carry out their activities under the responsibilities of the Quality\n              Assurance Programme of the CFE´s NCC and/or\n        2.    Contractors evaluated and qualified by the Quality Assurance Department of the\n              CFE´s NCC, to perform safety-related activities under their own Quality\n              Assurance Programme.\n\n        Also the following procedures are established:\n\n        -     General administrative process for contracting goods and services, and important\n              to safety works.\n        -     Participation of the Quality Assurance Department in the process of contracting\n              important to safety services.\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                            11-7\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        -     Guidelines for the receipt of work performed by contractors to ensure that all work\n              and its associated documentation are properly completed and delivered to CFE.\n\n\n   -    Methods used to assess the qualification and training of contractor’s\n        personnel\n\n        Contractor personnel who perform safety-related functions are required to present\n        evidence of the qualification of their personnel, which is evaluated by the Quality\n        Assurance Department and the contracting area. The qualification and training\n        requirements for such personnel are stipulated in the corresponding contract.\n\n   -    Methods used for the analysis of the competence, availability and\n        sufficiency of additional personnel required for severe accident\n        management, including contracted personnel or personnel from other\n        nuclear facilities\n\n        LVNPS has departments of Emergency Response Planning, an Internal Emergency\n        Response Organization and an Organization of Response to External Radiological\n        Emergencies. Internally, the organization covers the functions and requirements of\n        the regulatory framework, using operational procedures in emergencies and,\n        recently, the implementation of severe accident guidelines is in process (in\n        accordance with the recommendations of the BWR Owners Group).\n\n        The functions necessary for the response of external radiological emergencies are\n        covered by a committee composed of different federal and state government\n        agencies (including the LVNPS itself), which provide the qualified human resources\n        and materials necessary to cover the emergency response functions.\n\n   -    Description of the national supply of, and demand for, experts in nuclear\n        science and technology\n\n        As stated in the “Measures to develop and maintain competence” section of Article 8,\n        only one nuclear power plant is operated in the country. Although there have been\n        declarations regarding the construction of new reactors, there are no formal requests\n        for licensing, so the demand for professionals in nuclear engineering and science\n        areas is low, even considering the requirements of all the entities involved, such as the\n        operator of the LVNPS, the Regulatory Body and research institutes. The supply of\n        educational programs and graduates in the nuclear area is consequently also low. On\n        the other engineering areas (mechanical, electrical, civil, chemical, and so on.), which\n        constitute the largest percentage of professionals to operate or regulate a nuclear\n        facility, the offer of academic programs and graduates is considerable; however, their\n        training is directed only to non-nuclear conventional systems.\n\n        Additionally, the CFE has established linkage mechanisms with universities and\n        technological colleges for the incorporation of personnel from different engineering\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                            11-8\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        areas, carrying out specialized introductory courses on nuclear energy. This is done in\n        order to facilitate the entrance of graduates to the nuclear power plant's work force.\n        In the last two years, graduates from national universities and technology centers with\n        degrees in nuclear engineering or specialties related to radiation protection have\n        been invited to participate in the selection process for supervisors in the areas of\n        reactor engineering, radiation protection and chemical engineering.\n\n   -    Regulatory review and control activities\n\n        CNSNS evaluates the following: (1) on an annual basis - the revisions of the initial\n        training and retraining programmes of the personnel required in 10 CFR 50.120\n        (Systematic Approach to Training) and the personnel performing safety-related\n        activities; (2) on semi-annual basis - the performance of the technical staff of the areas\n        of maintenance (electrical, mechanical and, instrumentation and control), chemistry,\n        radiation protection, plant engineering, design engineering, reactor engineering and\n        Field Assistant Operators, and (3) on a quarterly basis - the performance of Reactor\n        and Senior Reactor Operators, as well as the performance of the LVNPS full-scope\n        simulator. This evaluation activity is performed in compliance with Mexican Official\n        Standard NOM-034-NUCL-2016 \"Selection, qualification and training requirements of\n        personnel in nuclear power plant\", and it is documented through the \"Minutes and\n        Report on the Evaluation of Conformity with NOM-034-NUCL-2016\".\n\n        As part of the control measures established by CNSNS is the preparation of the\n        following: (1) the semi-annual results report on the implementation of the Systematic\n        Approach to Training for the personnel specified in 10 CFR 50.120 and reactor\n        engineering; (2) the quarterly results report on the retraining cycles of Reactor and\n        Senior Reactor Operators; (3) the annual results report on the evaluation of ROs and\n        SROs; (4) the biennial programme of retraining for the ROs and SROs; and (5) the\n        annual programme of retraining for the simulator instructors. These reports are\n        presented by the LVNPS authorisation holder to CNSNS.\n\n        The reports mentioned above are reviewed by the specialist staff of CNSNS. The\n        purpose of the review is to verify that the qualification and training requirements of\n        personnel performing a key function for the safe operation of nuclear installations are\n        met at all times. Recently, the CNSNS initiated the \"evaluation\" of the LVNPS reactor\n        operators and supervisors' requalification program by means of the following\n        indicators: (1) at least 75% of the operators and supervisors must approve all sections\n        of the exam in the participant (written and simulator), and (2) at least two thirds (66%)\n        of the operating shifts must pass the simulator exam.\n\n        For the case of contractors’ personnel, CNSNS has established in the Mexican Official\n        Standards the specific requirements. It is required that the staff of those companies\n        or organisations engaged in any activity or function for more than a year must meet\n        all regulatory requirements for such activity or function.\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. The verification is performed using one of the\n        following options:\n\n        1.    Contractor documents which were used to qualify its personnel to perform the\n              assigned task;\n        2.    Previous verification of the ability of the contractor's personnel to perform the task\n              assigned, by personnel of the nuclear installation;\n        3.    Successful completion by the contractor's personnel of those licence holder´s\n              training programme segments regarded as necessary to perform the task\n              assigned.\n\n\n   -    Actions taken by CNSNS to manage risks related to the Covid-19 pandemic\n\n\n        In connection with the examination program, the CNSNS implemented the following\n        actions:\n        1.   Rescheduling operational tests on the simulator located at the site.\n        2. Approved a time relief for training during this period.\n\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. the report states such inspections should occur if organizational or equipment changes arise.", "question_ids": ["1B.10", "1B.3"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "Directly covers 2019-2021, with implications for continuing oversight in 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "The source attributes the inspection decision and framework to CNSNS; The objective of this requirement is that the LVNPS staff playing a vital\n        role in the efficient and safe generation of electric power (e.g., operators and\n        maintenance personnel) operate, verify and reset the plant equipment and its\n        components in such a way that the causal factors of human error are eliminated as\n        much as possible.\n\n        Consistent with this requirement, a general guideline was also established that in\n        order to comply with 10 CFR 50.34(f)(2)(iii), LVNPS should follow the directions of the\n        Standard Review Plan Chapter 18, NUREG-0800 (USNRC, 2004), which in combination\n        with the Human Factors Engineering Programme Review Model described in\n        NUREG-0711, provide detailed criteria to be considered in a Human Factors\n        Programme.\n\n        As a result of the implementation of all tasks required in the Standard Review Plan\n        Chapter 18, the CNSNS reviewed the LVNPS compliance with the human factors\n        engineering principles. Also, to ensure that the conditions to minimise human errors\n        were maintained, The CNSNS imposed two requirements for Unit 2 in its current\n        LVNPS Commercial Operation Authorisation in order to keep this authorisation in\n        force:\n\n        a)     Requirement No. 2 establishes that the LVNPS simulator should meet the\n               requirements of fidelity, response and reliability. Likewise, the simulator shall be\n               equipped with the Safety Parameters Display System.\n        b)     Requirement No. 6 establishes that LVNPS must continue with the Alarm\n               Reduction Programme in the Main Control Room, implementing the necessary\n               design changes.\n        In the case of Unit 1, its Operating Permit, renewed until 2050, no longer explicitly\n        describes the above, as this is embedded in compliance with Condition No. 4, which\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Continue the practices suggested in the “Human Factors Engineering Program\n               Review Model” described in NUREG-0711 whose recommendations can and\n               must be applied during the design, construction and operation of nuclear\n               installations.\n\n               According to this review model, the inclusion of the Human Factors Engineering\n               principles was verified in several CFE activities by reviewing the following items:\n\n               a.     Operational experience - CNSNS inspections systematically consider\n                      operational experience, as does review of the condition reports, in which\n                      the involvement of operational experience is documented. In addition, an\n                      evaluation of operational experience, as established in the basic inspection\n                      programme, is performed every two years. During these activities the\n                      External and Internal Operational Experience evaluation and feedback\n                      process is reviewed.\n               b.     Man-machine interface design - During examinations for new operator\n                      licences and renewals for Reactor Operators (RO) and Senior Reactor\n                      Operator (SRO), the man-machine interface was continually evaluated in\n                      order to identify human error precursors caused by the new controls and\n                      operator aids, and to make changes in the Main Control Room if necessary.\n               c.     Training Programme - CNSNS continually follows the analysis,\n                      development, implementation, evaluation, and feedback processes of the\n                      Systematic Approach to Training. This approach considers the knowledge\n                      and skills that the staff must have to safely perform a task.\n        2.     The root cause analysis of operational events has improved the identification of\n               causal factors of human error, such as: environmental conditions; interface with\n               equipment design or condition; verbal and written communication; training\n               methods and qualification of personnel; planning and work practices;\n               supervision methods; and administrative methods. The above has allowed to\n               fully identify the factors that originated the human error.\n\n\n        Regarding the inspection program, as mentioned in Article 7, during the period\n        covered by this National Report, no inspections were carried out on activities related\n        to human and organizational factors at the Laguna Verde Nuclear Power Plant Units\n        1 and 2. The\n        training evaluation is done as follows.\n\n        a)     Theoretical training\n\n               At the end of the training session, the trainees are evaluated by a written\n               examination to demonstrate that the necessary knowledge to carry out the\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. The first is a “supervised practice,” during\n               which trainees (staff) perform activities based on a planned scenario, making use\n               of the resources available to them. The training supervisor can stop the activity\n               to make observations and to show how the function should be performed.\n\n               In the second stage, carried out without the interaction with the supervisor, the\n               trainees are required to perform an activity, one that is part of the emergency\n               plan, for an accident scenario that is unknown to them until the session begins.\n               They need to use all available material resources to perform the activity, and each\n               trainee is evaluated according to his or her performance, which must be in\n               adherence to procedures and the operations manual, specifically taking into\n               account the critical steps to satisfactorily perform the activity.\n\n        Both the theoretical and practical training are evaluated following the LVNPS internal\n        procedures.\n\n        Main results\n\n        During the 2019-2021 period, the Said exercise\n        was evaluated by the CNSNS, identifying 78 findings to the various Task Forces that\n        compose it, without these representing a risk to the population during the response\n        to a General Emergency at the plant. Currently, the Task Forces are working on the\n        implementation of the modifications, and carrying out the relevant training, prior to\n        the integrated exercise that was planned for 2021; however, the exercise was\n        rescheduled for 2022 at the request of the CFE and the National Civil Protection\n        Coordination.\n\n   -    International arrangements, including those with neighbouring States, as\n        necessary.\n\n        See Annexes 7.1 and 7.2 of this National Report.\n\nArticle 16 (2)        Information to the public and neighbouring states\n\n   -    Overview of the Contracting Party’s arrangements for informing the public\n        in the vicinity of the nuclear installations about emergency planning and\n        emergency situations\n\n        One of the key factors for a successful implementation of the measures to protect the\n        population living in a radius of up to 16 km from LVNPS is the information regarding\n        their participation in the event of a radiological emergency.\n\n        The main information media are the following:\n\n        a)     The EREP calendar, which is distributed in all homes and businesses located in a\n               radius of up to 16 km from LVNPS. The calendar has information on actions to be\n               completed by the population in case of an emergency. This information is\n               delivered to about 4,000 households.\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           16-22\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        b)     Visits of communities to the LVNPS Information Centre. The Information Centre\n               makes the invitation and provides transportation for the community groups. The\n               communities have the opportunity to go to the centre and receive information\n               about the External Radiological Emergency Plan. This programme receives an\n               average of 300 people per year.\n        c)     Visits to schools around LVNPS. Over the past three years, information about\n               LVNPS and the EREP has been shared with more than 1,700 students.\n\n\n   -    Arrangements to inform competent authorities in neighbouring States, as\n        necessary\n\n        Since 1988, Mexico has been a part of the “Convention on Early Notification of a\n        Nuclear Accident” and the “Convention on Assistance in the Case of a Nuclear\n        Accident or Radiological Emergency”. Based on these conventions, there is an\n        established procedure to declare a radiological emergency at a Mexican nuclear\n        installation if an event has the likelihood of affecting neighbouring states. The\n        procedure’s objective, to provide early notification, and its application are the\n        responsibility of CNSNS. Notwithstanding the preceding, Mexico has bilateral\n        agreements for exchanging technical information (including the occurrence of\n        significant events) with their closest neighbours.\n\n        Mexico is also part of the IAEA Early Communications International System, which\n        would be used to immediately provide information to the IAEA in the event of a\n        radiological emergency at LVNPS. The same information would be available to\n        neighbouring states. Mexico is also a signatory of the “Vienna Convention on Civil\n        Liability for Nuclear Damage”. It is important to mention that the LVNPS site is over\n        500 km away from the border of the nearest country.\n\n        Mexico has a National System for Civil Protection. In the event of a radiological\n        emergency at a nuclear installation in Unites States of America, near the border with\n        Mexico, with the likelihood to affect the national territory, the DN-III Plan would be\n        activated. The Ministry of Defence is responsible to apply the DN-III Plan under the\n        direction of the Secretariat of the Interior, assisted by the Secretariat of Health and\n        DICONSA. The DN-III Plan includes the infrastructure required to establish adequate\n        communications, evacuation capacity and establishment of control points for taking\n        the appropriate actions during the emergency. However, it should be noted that the\n        United States nuclear power plants are sited more than 100 km from Mexico’s border\n        and other Central American countries such, as Guatemala, do not have this kind of\n        power stations.\n\n        Technical issues to be considered\n\n   -    Related to the Event at Japan’s Fukushima Daiichi Plant\n\n        In connection with the event that occurred at the Fukushima Daiichi plant in Japan\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Based on this assumption, the LVNPS developed strategies in which\n                     the response capacity is not compromised by the failure of any common\n                     system and/or equipment.\n\n              2)    Additional responsibilities of government agencies\n\n                     As mentioned before in this Article 16, with regard to the External\n                     Radiological Emergency Plan, the Organization for Emergencies Response\n                     is formed by the federal and state agencies listed previously, which form the\n                     External Radiological Emergency Plan Committee.\n\n              3)    New procedures\n\n                     LVNPS has developed the Severe Accident Management Guidelines (SAMG)\n                     based on the guidelines for the Emergency Procedures and for Severe\n                     Accidents EPG / SAG (Emergency Procedure Guidelines / Severe Accident\n                     Guidelines) Rev. 3 of the BWR Reactor Owners Group. The SAMGs coordinate\n                     the control of the key parameters of the plant under severe accident\n                     conditions, and provide the guidelines to flood the core and its slag.\n\n                     Additionally, support procedures (PDS) have been developed for the SAMGs,\n                     which contain the information to perform the necessary actions to carry out\n                     the alignments, connections and disconnections required for the monitoring\n                     of the SAMGs.\n\n              4)    Joint actions with various agencies and improvements in international\n                    cooperation\n\n                     Mexico, through CNSNS, participates in the Organisation for Economic Co-\n                     operation and Development’s (OECD) Nuclear Energy Agency (NEA) Senior\n                     Task Force on the “Impact of the Fukushima Daiichi Nuclear Plant Accident”.\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           16-24\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                  NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                     CNSNS is also a member of the Ibero-American Forum of Radiological and\n                     Nuclear Regulatory Agencies (FORO in Spanish), which has developed as one\n                     of its tasks, stress tests for nuclear power plants. The tests are similar to the\n                     ones defined by the European Nuclear Safety Regulators Group (ESREG) and\n                     the Western European Nuclear Regulators Association (WENRA).\n\n                     Regarding cooperation with international groups on this issue, the CFE is an\n                     active committee member within the BWR Reactor Owners Group\n                     (BWROG). This membership allows CFE to be prepared for events beyond\n                     the design basis in accordance with the fleet of reactors of the same type.\n                     The CFE´s interaction with committees such as the Fukushima Response\n                     Committee (FRC) and the Emergency Procedures Committee (EPC)\n                     facilitates the integrated response within the industry to the orders issued\n                     by the USNRC and endorsed by the CNSNS. Additionally, the interaction with\n                     plants of the same type allows the exchange of experiences with nuclear\n                     power plants that have already implemented the orders of the USNRC EA-\n                     12-049, EA-12-051 and EA-13-109, which have been required to theCNSNS.\n\n        Actions of the Regulatory Body\n\n        As a consequence of the accident at the Fukushima Daiichi nuclear power plant in\n        Japan, the CNSNS required the LVNPS to implement the necessary mitigation\n        strategies to comply with NRC Order EA-12-049 (Flex) during the initial, transition and\n        final response phases, to address beyond design basis accidents; as well as, the\n        development and implementation of procedures and guidelines for CFE's response\n        to these accidents.\n\n        In response to the above, CFE acquired additional equipment and material to comply\n        with the Order and reevaluated the areas established as Response Centers, in order\n        to guarantee the protection of the members of the emergency response team, due\n        to the leakage of radioactive material.\n\n        As a result, the following actions were taken at the LVNPS:\n\n        1.    The location for the storage of materials and equipment necessary for CFE's\n              emergency response was determined, which corresponds to the Flex Building,\n              which complies with the characteristics required by Order EA-12-049.\n        2.    A new Technical Support Center (CAT, Abbreviation in Spanish), called \"Alternate\n              CAT\" was assigned for the Internal Emergency Plan (IEP), located in the Integral\n              Process Information System building, which will be used in the event that the\n              support centres of both units of the LVNPS are disabled.\n        3.    The amount of potassium iodide tablets needed was reevaluated in order to have\n              a sufficient reserve for the emergency response personnel and the population\n              living within the 16 km zone, in case radiological prophylaxis is required.\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Actions to improve LVNPS’s response to an ELAP\n\n                 As part of its commitments with CNSNS, CFE has implemented the following\n                 mitigation strategies to strengthen the response to an ELAP:\n\n\n                     LVNPS has evaluated its Coping Time with an ELAP, and has concluded that\n                      using procedures to cut non-essential loads, the duration of the batteries is\n                      8 hours.\n                     The mitigation strategies in compliance with NEI 12-06 Rev. 2, as well as NEI\n                      12-02 Rev. 1 and NEI 13-02 Rev 1. Diversification of Spent Fuel Pool level instrumentation and inventory\n                            replenishment alternatives.\n                       ii. Portable power supply for reactor vessel depressurisation and water\n                           injection with a portable pump.\n                       iii. Manual operation of the containment hardened vent system.\n                       iv. The diesel generators currently installed at LVNPS will be electrically\n                           interconnected, in order to supply power to the direct-current battery\n                           chargers:\n                            1.   For Unit 1 the Central Alarm Station DG was connected to a new\n                                 Motor Control Center (MCC) installed on level 10.15 at the Control\n                                 Building to power the 125 and 250 DCV battery chargers.\n\n                            2. In the case of Unit 2, the DG of the Integrated Process Information\n                               System (SIIP Abbreviation in Spanish) was connected to the new\n                               MCC installed on level 10.15 at the Control Building. As in the case of\n                               Unit 1, this interconnection will contribute to powering the 125 and\n                               250 VDC battery chargers.\n\n                            3. Additionally, a quick connection box was installed in both units to\n                               connect the MCC to a Portable Diesel Generator to power the 125\n                               and 250 VDC battery chargers.\n\n\n        G.     Emergency preparedness and response improvements\n\n        This information is presented in Article 16.\n\n\n        H.     Implementation of diverse and flexible coping strategies (FLEX)\n\n        As mentioned, CFE is committed to complying with the order of the NRC EA-12-049\n        through the guidelines established in the NEI 12-06 Rev. 2. Such compliance commits\n        LVNPS to have alternatives diverse and flexible to maintain the cooling capacities of\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener                Period 2019-2021\n                                                           18-17\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        the core, cooling of the Spent Fuel Pool and the integrity of the Primary Containment\n        before an event beyond the design bases that lead both LVNPS Units to Extended\n        Loss of AC Power (ELAP) and Loss of Normal Access to the Ultimate Heat Sink (UHS)\n        condition.\n\n        There are three phases of the mitigation strategy:\n\n              1.    In the first phase, the event must be faced, making use of the equipment\n                    installed at LVNPS.\n              2. The second phase involves transitioning from using installed equipment to on-\n                 site portable equipment.\n              3. In the third phase, additional response capacity must be obtained and\n                 redundant portable equipment from off-site must be utilized until electric\n                 power, the inventory of water, and the coolant injection systems have been\n                 restored.\n\n        On the three phases required for the FLEX strategies, LVNPS has developed the\n        procedures for compliance with Phase 1 and Phase 2, and is making the last\n        modifications to satisfy the requirements of Phase 2. Phase 3 of these strategies are\n        still in development.\n\n        I.         Severe Accident Management Guidelines\n\n        The CFE has developed the Severe Accidents Management Guidelines (SAMG) based\n        on the guidelines for the Emergency Procedure Guidelines / Severe Accident\n        Guidelines EPG / SAG Rev. 3 of the BWR Owners Group. These guidelines were\n        developed in response to NUREG-0737. Item IC1 \"Guidance for the evaluation and\n        Development of Procedures for Transients and Accidents\" and NEI 91-04 \"Severe\n        Accident Issue Closure Guidelines\", Rev. 1, respectively. So CFE has developed its\n        Emergency Procedures and Severe Accident Guides adapting them to the LVNPS\n        particular characteristics.\n\n        The SAMGs coordinate the control of the key parameters of the plant under severe\n        accident conditions, and provide guidelines for flooding the core and its slag. These\n        guidelines are sustained in the Support Procedures which contain the information to\n        perform the necessary actions to carry out the alignments, connections and\n        disconnections required for the SAMG monitoring.\n\n        J.         Regulatory Body (CNSNS) Actions\n\n        With respect to the actions programmed for the LVNPS in order to meet the\n        regulatory requirements established by the CNSNS, to respond to an event of the\n        magnitude of the impact to the Fukushima Daiichi power plant, during the period\n        covered by this Report National, the Regulatory Body held quarterly meetings with\n        the CFE in order to verify compliance with its program of activities.\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           18-18\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        As a result of the review of the actions carried out by the CFE, the following is\n        described:\n\n        With respect to the update of the Probabilistic Safety Analysis (PSA) Level 2, in its\n        second phase, the CFE issued the 2014 version. As a result of the evaluation made by\n        the CNSNS to the responses issued by the CFE to the Requirements for Additional\n        Information (RAI), CNSNS determined that these analyzes were performed in\n        accordance with the accepted methodologies under best practices and the results\n        identify early stage releases. Therefore, CFE will be requested an update program\n        based on the modifications that are consistent with the orders required by the NRC,\n        to counteract the vulnerability of early release.\n\n        Regarding the actions required by regulation 10 CFR 50.54 (hh) 2), CNSNS verified its\n        implementation in the construction stages in accordance with the modification\n        packages generated by the CFE to comply with this regulation. It is currently pending\n        the conclusion report delivery to the Regulatory Body.\n\n        For the mitigation strategies of events beyond the design bases, CNSNS authorized\n        CFE to implement the modification packages required for the implementation of the\n        Hard Venting order in its 2nd Stage of Phase 1 in the LVNPS Units 1 and 2. With respect\n        to the reliable instrumentation order for the spent fuel pool, the CNSNS witnessed the\n        implementation of the corresponding modification packages, determining that the\n        construction phase was completed. Finally, with regard to the FLEX Order, the CFE\n        established the strategies to be carried out during the 1st and 2nd phase of response\n        of the LVNPS, before events beyond the design bases, pending the definition of the\n        response of the 3rd phase by the CFE. In addition to the above, the CNSNS required\n        the CFE to provide comprehensive evaluation reports on each of the previous orders,\n        to verify compliance with the provisions of the regulatory framework.\n\n        As for the Severe Accident Management Guidelines (SAMG), CFE has informed CNSNS\n        on time, of the progress that BWROG is making regarding the release of the Extensive\n        Damage Mitigation Guidelines (EDMG). On the other hand, regarding the Emergency\n        Procedures based on the EPG / SAG Rev. 3 of the BWROG and its Support Procedures,\n        CNSNS has verified that these procedures are included in the Retraining Program of\n        Licensed Personnel.\n\n        Finally, CNSNS prepared, in May 2017, the National Report on Assessment Report on\n        Stress Tests at the Laguna Verde Nuclear Power Plant. This report recapitulates the\n        activities carried out in Mexico, regarding to the whole process related to the tests\n        stress. In this report, the updates implemented in the LVNPS are also integrated, since\n        the last meeting of experts-members of the Ibero-American Forum of Radiological\n        and Nuclear Regulatory Agencies (FORO in Spanish). Additionally, good practices and\n        experiences identified during the process are included, in order to exchange them\n        with the other regulatory bodies of the region.\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX.
