It is important to mention that the turbine generator set originally\n        installed in LVNPS units was designed, manufactured and supplied by Mitsubishi\n        Heavy Industries who supported all the time the modifications and major\n        maintenance while its equipment was in operation. With the Extended Power Uprate\n        project for a power uprate up to 120% of the RTP, the turbo-generator set of both\n        LVNPS units was replaced with ALSTOM equipment. General Electric recently\n        acquired ALSTOM with whom a relationship is maintained of technical support for\n        LVNPS.\n\n   -    Regulatory review and control activities\n\n        As mentioned in Article 14, CNSNS verifies annually the Engineering and Technical\n        Support areas through its Baseline Inspection Programme. During refuelling outages,\n        CNSNS also verifies that the service suppliers meet the following: their Quality\n        Assurance Programmes or they are included in LVNPS‘s QAP; comply with the\n        training and qualifications required for the activities that will develop; the Radiation\n        Protection and Industrial Safety controls established by LVNPS.\n\nArticle 19 (6)        Reporting of incidents significant to safety\n\n   -    Overview of the Contracting Party’s arrangements and regulatory\n        requirements to report incidents significant to safety to the regulatory\n        body\n\n        According to the regulatory framework, LVNPS must notify to CNSNS the occurrence\n        of all incidents covered by categories defined in 10 CFR 20.2202, 10 CFR 20.2203, 10 CFR\n        50.72, 10 CFR 50.73 and NUREG-1022 Rev. 3. The notification is sent to the CNSNS\n        Resident Inspector at LVNPS and, and depending on the classification of the event\n        within: 1 hour, 4 hours and 8 hours.\n\n        Subsequently, and within a period not exceeding 24 hours of the event, LVNPS must\n        send to the CNSNS headquarters, a notification through the format identified as\n        Notification of Reportable Event (NRE). Such format includes a summary of the event;\n        immediate corrective actions; status of the core emergency cooling system and\n        engineering safeguards system conditions; as well as information on radiological\n        conditions as applicable.\n\n        Finally, and within 60 days after the event occurred, LVNPS must send to the CNSNS\n        a Licensee Event Report (LER), where the event is fully described, the result of the root\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-21\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        cause analysis and the proposed corrective and preventive actions, in order to avoid\n        recurrence.\n\n   -    Overview of the established reporting criteria and reporting procedures for\n        incidents significant to safety and other events such as near misses and\n        accidents\n\n        The notification and reporting of the LVNPS safety important events are carried out\n        according to the notification and reporting criteria set in the Procedure \"LVNPS Event\n        Notification and Reporting to CNSNS\". This procedure is based on 10 CFR 20.2202, 10\n        CFR 20.2203, 10 CFR 50.72, 10 CFR 50.73, and NUREG-1022 \"Event Reporting Guidelines:\n        10 CFR 50.72 and 50.73\".\n\n   -    Statistics of reported incidents significant to safety for the past three years\n\n        Figure 19.2 shows a plot for the events that have occurred in both LVNPS units from\n        the start of operation through 2021.\n\n        The most important events at LVNPS during the 2019-2021 period are described\n        below.\n\n             1.   Manual SCRAM of the Reactor due to loss of vacuum in the Main Condenser.\n\n                  On February 03, 2019, the manual SCRAM of the Unit 2 Reactor was performed\n                  due to the loss of vacuum of the Main Condenser during the application of the\n                  procedure for the Main Turbine Valve Test, requiring putting into service the\n                  loop A of the Residual Heat Removal System (RHR) to cool the Suppression Pool\n                  and subsequently, manually put into service the Reactor Core Isolation Cooling\n                  System (RCIC) to control the level and pressure of the Reactor.\n\n                  The cause of the event was that the failed section of pipe was made of carbon\n                  steel material instead of chrome-molybdenum, which was confirmed in the\n                  16th Unit 2 refueling by metallographic analysis.\n\n             2. Room No. RX-211 unlocked, this being a High Radiation Area.\n\n                  On February 12, 2019, during the preparations for the leak test to the return\n                  valve of the Cooling and Cleaning System of the Suppression Pool of Unit 1, the\n                  Predictive Maintenance personnel reported to the Reactor checkpoint\n                  technician that the barrier to controlling the access to the High Radiation Area\n                  was not in place.\n\n                  The cause of this event was that a double check was not carried out to ensure\n                  that access to the room was closed and secured, since the annexe of the\n                  Radiation Protection procedure does not have a specific field to document\n                  such simultaneous verification.\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-22\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n             3. Unit 2 Reactor Automatic SCRAM due to Main Turbine Trip.\n\n                 On April 09, 2019, occurred the actuation of both channels of the Reactor\n                 Protection System (RPS) by the trip of the Main Turbine, upon actuation of a\n                 component of the Main Generator. In this event, all systems operated as\n                 designed.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that the new requirements for digital components\n                 were not reviewed so in Unit 2 these components were still required to operate\n                 at a temperature that no longer corresponded to them.\n\n             4. Isolation of the Residual Heat Removal (RHR) System in its Standby Cooling\n                mode.\n\n                 On April 22, 2019, after starting the RHR System in its Shutdown Cooling mode,\n                 the isolation of this system occurred due to the actuation of an instrument,\n                 closing an external isolation valve.\n\n                 The cause of the event was the gradual accumulation of dirt and rust in the\n                 low- pressure line of the actuated instrument.\n\n             5. Startup by human error of the main pump of the low-pressure Core Spray\n                (LPCS) System during the application of a maintenance procedure.\n\n                 On April 15, 2019, while performing a maintenance procedure on a valve of the\n                 Residual Heat Removal (RHR) System heat exchanger \"A\", Unit 2, the automatic\n                 startup of the LPCS main pump occurred. The maintenance technician\n                 reported that he misplaced a jumper, resulting in the system startup.\n\n                 The root cause of this event was a failure to improve Electrical Maintenance to\n                 identify and raise awareness of the risk involved in working on critical\n                 components or critical activities.\n\n             6. Manual start of pump \"B\", Division I, of the Nuclear Service Water (NSW) System\n                due to low pressure and low flow of pump \"A\", Division I, of the same system.\n\n                 On May 18, 2019, low flow and low-pressure alarms were present in the NSW\n                 System, pump \"A\" division I, of Unit 2. Subsequently, pump \"B\" was manually\n                 started, restoring the nominal pressure and flow conditions in division I of this\n                 system.\n\n                 The causes of the event were inadequate risk analysis of the diving activity in\n                 the suction bay of the system, inadequate use of error prevention tools and\n                 weaknesses in the equipment restriction control.\n\n             7. Automatic SCRAM of the Reactor by actuation of both Reactor Protection\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-23\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 System channels.\n\n                 On October 02, 2019, automatic actuation of both channels of the Unit 2 Reactor\n                 Protection System occurred, with automatic opening of a Safety and Relief\n                 Valve (SRV). The Reactor Water Cleanup (RWCU) system was isolated due to a\n                 disturbance in an instrument, the Reactor was manually isolated due to a rapid\n                 decrease in Reactor pressure. The Reactor Core Isolated Core Cooling (RCIC)\n                 System was started for reactor level and pressure control, and the Residual\n                 Heat Removal (RHR) system was started to cool the Suppression Pool. Two SRV\n                 valves were opened for Reactor pressure control.\n\n                 The cause of the event was a deficiency in the operation of the system, which\n                 occurs at a level where users do not have access to make modifications or\n                 changes, since the failure corresponds to a level of the design developer. This\n                 type of control system should be updated based on reviews, operational\n                 experiences of users and investigations by the manufacturer.\n\n             8. Room No. TB-313 unlocked, this being a High Radiation Area.\n\n                 On November 16, 2019, Unit 1 was operating at 85% power following routine\n                 power reduction activities, Physical Security personnel notified the Radiation\n                 Protection (RP) technician on duty that the door leading to room TB-313\n                 (considered a High Radiation Area) was closed; however, the padlock was\n                 poorly placed and did not secure the lock on the pin. Physical Security\n                 personnel took control, remaining in the area to prevent access. Room TB-313\n                 was finally secured; however, there is an administrative record that the door\n                 should have been secured approximately two hours earlier.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that the RP technician omitted to assign a\n                 custodian with whom he would perform the double verification of the door\n                 security, as it should be done in High Radiation Areas.\n\n                 Once the dose records were reviewed, it was concluded that there were no\n                 personnel entering the room in question, so there was no dose to personnel\n                 due to unplanned exposure.\n\n             9. Automatic Reactor SCRAM due to Turbine trip.\n\n                 On November 30, 2019, there was an automatic tripping of both channels of the\n                 Unit 2 Reactor Protection System, due to the Turbine trip, which was caused, in\n                 turn, by the trip of the Main Generator due to the trip of a relay, leading finally\n                 to the automatic opening of a Safety and Relief Valve (SRV).\n\n                 The root cause was that the design review during the Extended Power Uprate\n                 (EPU) project did not identify the conditions under which the heating elements\n                 should operate without challenging or stressing the service components.\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener              Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-24\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n             10. Operation of Unit 2 with 100.3% power.\n\n                 On January 09, 2020, while Unit 2 was in a stable condition, trip alarms from the\n                 hydraulic power unit \"B\" of the Recirculation System (RRC) occurred; when it\n                 got back in service an increase in the loop \"B\" flow and decrease in the position\n                 indication of the loop flow control valve was observed. An unsuccessful attempt\n                 was made to decrease the loop \"B\" flow. Later on, it was observed that the\n                 power was above 100% of the Nominal Power with a value of 100.3%, so it was\n                 proceeded to decrease the power with the flow control valve \"A\" until reaching\n                 97% of power.\n\n                 The cause of the event was the fracture and fatigue of a coupling since it had\n                 been installed for more than 20 years.\n\n             11. The Reactor Core Isolation Cooling (RCIC) System inoperable due to velocity\n                 and flow oscillations during the application of a procedure.\n\n                 On October 04, 2020, when applying an operating verification procedure, the\n                 turbine of the RCIC system of Unit 1 was put into service and the speed was\n                 adjusted with the corresponding controller in manual mode, according to the\n                 instructions of the same procedure. Subsequently, the controller was put in\n                 automatic mode and then speed and flow oscillations occurred, so the\n                 verification was suspended and declared unsatisfactory. Later on, the RCIC\n                 system turbine was put on standby according to the same procedure, with no\n                 oscillations in the control when the controller was put on automatic.\n\n                 The root cause of this event was a deficiency during change control\n                 management in maintenance tests, since the gain of the RCIC system flow\n                 controller was modified during post-maintenance tests, having different\n                 system parameters from the nominal ones, without documenting them\n                 adequately, and finally having an erroneous gain value.\n\n             12. Reactor Manual SCRAM due to Recirculation System pump \"B\" trip.\n\n                 On November 07, 2020, during the low speed transfer of the Unit 2 recirculation\n                 pumps, according to a system operating procedure, pump \"B\" did not transfer\n                 to low speed, so a manual SCRAM of the Reactor was performed according to\n                 an abnormal operation procedure.\n\n                 The most probable cause of the event is that due to a lack of attention to detail\n                 during the maneuver, the operator did not properly manipulate the position of\n                 the handles, as he did not observe all the required parameters.\n\n             13. Failure of explosive valve \"A\" of the Traversing Incore Probe (TIP) during the\n                 application of a procedure for valves and pumps.\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-25\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 On December 1, 2020, during the periodic operability test of the explosive\n                 charges of the TIP system valves, the charge corresponding to valve \"A\" did not\n                 detonate.\n\n                 The direct cause of the event was a deformation in the explosive valve\n                 connector. The root cause of the event was a situation not covered by the\n                 respective procedure for valves and pumps, since, although it is performed step\n                 by step, it did not consider in detail the sequence of connection and\n                 disconnection of the TIP valve connector, which would prevent damage to this\n                 connector.\n\n             14. Trip of the Main/Normal Auxiliary Transformer and satisfactory startup of the\n                 Diesel Generators Div. I and Div. II.\n\n                 On December 20, 2020, the Main/Normal Auxiliary Transformer tripped,\n                 resulting in a loss of external power due to the absence of the Standby\n                 Transformer (T23), and consequently the automatic startup of the Diesel\n                 Generators Div. I and Div. II of Unit 2 occurred satisfactorily.\n\n                 As a consequence of the power loss, pump \"A\" of the Residual Heat Removal\n                 (RHR) System and pump \"A\" of the Fuel Pool Cooling and Cleaning System\n                 tripped and were immediately restored to service. Reactor cavity draining was\n                 suspended in accordance with a general operating procedure and an Unusual\n                 Event was declared.\n\n                 The Substation Operator reported that the Main Transformer protections were\n                 tripped due to an error by the Electrical Protections personnel, so that after the\n                 electrical protections were restored, the Main/Normal Auxiliary Transformer\n                 was energized and, therefore, the non-critical 4.16 kV buses were energized,\n                 ending the emergency.\n\n                 The root cause of this event was that the electrical maintenance personnel did\n                 not conduct an effective pre-work meeting, so the error prevention tools were\n                 not properly used to perform the task.\n\n             15. Automatic Reactor SCRAM due to Main Turbine trip.\n\n                 On January 20, 2021, there was an automatic trip of both channels of the\n                 Reactor Protection System (RPS) due to the trip of the Main Turbine of Unit 1,\n                 which was caused by a relay trip. There was a fast transfer of auxiliaries from\n                 transformer T11 to transformer T12, automatic opening of two Safety and Relief\n                 Valves (SRV) and the Alternate Rod Insertion (ARI) actuation.\n\n                 During the bypass actuation, a high Reactor level and the tripping of the\n                 feedwater Turbo-pump \"A\" occurred, which was reset for reactor level control.\n                 The Reactor was manually isolated at 35 kg/cm2 for pressure control according\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-26\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                 NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 to an abnormal operating procedure.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that a preventive maintenance procedure for\n                 transformer T11 did not contain instructions for the inspection and replacement\n                 of seals of the conduit boxes of the protection circuit cables, which led to the\n                 degradation of the seal of the conduit covers (conduit and accessories), causing\n                 the entry of water that, in chronic contact with the cables, led to the accelerated\n                 degradation of the wiring PVC insulation.\n\n             16. Unit 1 shutdown declared by Technical Specifications (TS).\n\n                 On January 22, 2021, a low count was observed in monitor \"E\" of the\n                 Startup/Intermediate Range Neutron Monitor (SRNM) System. Then, due to the\n                 failure of monitor \"E\", coinciding with the failure of monitor \"G\", the action of TS\n                 3.3.1 was taken and channel \"A\" of the Reactor Protection System (RPS) was\n                 tripped. Subsequently, the shutdown was declared by the TS. Finally, the\n                 Reactor Mode Selector was switched to \"Off\" and Operating Condition 3 \"Hot\n                 Standby\" was reached.\n\n                 The root cause of the event was an Instrumentation and Control maintenance\n                 procedure that contained two instructions in a single step, which caused the\n                 Instrumentation and Control (I&C) personnel to omit the second instruction to\n                 deliver the data obtained to the Engineering organization for graphing and\n                 analysis, preventing them from identifying the failure or degradation in the “E”\n                 and “G” monitors and determining their replacement during the 20th\n                 Refueling.\n\n             17. Failure to close a circuit breaker, during the application of an operating\n                 verification procedure of the Diesel Generator Div. III.\n\n                 On May 24, 2021, in Unit 2, during the scheduled execution of an operating\n                 verification procedure, having conditions to synchronize, the breaker of the\n                 Diesel Generator Div. III did not close.\n\n                 The root cause of the event was that the respective preventive maintenance\n                 procedure of the mentioned breaker did not detail the instructions to\n                 effectively perform the calibration of the trip arm of this breaker.\n\n             18. Failure of Division III essential cooling pump.\n\n                 On September 29, 2021, in Unit 1, the Division III essential cooling pump was put\n                 into service in accordance with a systems operating procedure, due to the\n                 injection of biocide into the Nuclear Service Water (NSW) System bays, with the\n                 general assistant reporting normal operation of the pump. Ten minutes later,\n                 there were alarms in the Main Control Room (MCR) on two panels due to the\n                 tripping of the feeder breaker of the Division III essential cooling pump. On\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener               Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-27\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 preliminary inspection of the equipment, the general assistant reported the\n                 characteristic odor of overheating and evidence of damage in the motor\n                 windings.\n\n                 The most likely cause of this event was a degradation of the motor winding\n                 insulation due to moisture/salinity ingress, as evidence was found of\n                 overheating in the stator winding due to a short circuit fault in the motor stator.\n                 As an immediate preventative action, a cover was placed on the marine ladder\n                 opening of the man inlet on the pump room floor to minimize moisture ingress.\n\n             19. Reactor manual SCRAM due to decreasing the period in the Startup Range\n                 Neutron Monitors in Operating Condition 2 \"Startup\".\n\n                 On October 22, 2021, during the shutdown of Unit 2, while in Operating\n                 Condition 2 \"Startup\" (approx. at 1% power), the reactor pressure began to\n                 decrease when the main turbine bypass valves were closed. During the course\n                 of the pressure decrease, a reduction in the reactor period was observed, so it\n                 was decided to trip the reactor manually.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that the Reactor Operator did not use the respective\n                 system operating procedure when performing the main steam realignment to\n                 ejector \"A\" and the supervision did not ensure that the activity was performed\n                 according to said procedure.\n\n             20. Startup of Safeguard Systems due to Main Transformer T2 trip, without fast\n                 transfer of auxiliaries.\n\n                 On October 30, 2021, in Unit 2, the main transformer (T2) tripped due to a relay\n                 trip, without the automatic transfer of power to the non-critical buses and\n                 critical buses, so the automatic startup of the Emergency Diesel Generators Div.\n                 I / II / III was initiated, energizing the critical buses and taking their loads\n                 satisfactorily, starting the emergency filter trains of the Main Control Room Div.\n                 I / II. Pump \"A\" of the Residual Heat Removal System (RHR) was tripped in\n                 Shutdown Cooling Mode and once the non-critical buses were energized from\n                 transformer T23, the pump was put back in service.\n\n                 The root cause of this event was the lack of attention to detail during the\n                 development of the electrical modification packages used to change the\n                 protections during the Extended Power Uprate (EPU) project due to the\n                 excessive amount of work. The EPU was an atypical project that exceeded the\n                 attention capacity in some areas of the Engineering Department. After the EPU\n                 was completed, a procedure was created for the planning and evaluation of\n                 future macro projects at the power plant, in order to improve the planning and\n                 scheduling of activities.\n\n             21. Failure of a spring-loading switch, during application of a procedure.\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener              Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-28\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n                 On November 30, 2021, in Unit 1, during the functional test, the Diesel Generator\n                 Div. III was unloaded and the machine breaker was opened according to the\n                 operating verification procedure. During the application of the checklist of the\n                 aforementioned procedure and by local indication of spring loading, it was\n                 determined that the aforementioned breaker had some unloaded springs,\n                 therefore the Diesel Generator Div. III was declared inoperable.\n\n                 On December 27, 2021, during the following functional test, the event occurred\n                 again, which was in the process of analysis.\n\n                 The cause of the event was that, during the preparation and review of the\n                 maintenance procedure in 2004, relevant information on the mechanical\n                 adjustments to be inspected and parts to be lubricated in the circuit breaker\n                 was not included, as indicated in the vendor's manual. Additionally, in the\n                 process of reviewing and issuing procedures, it was not ensured that all the\n                 detailed information was included.\n\n   -    Documentation and publication of reported events and incidents by both\n        the licence holders and regulatory body\n\n        Licence holders\n\n        The notification and reporting of LVNPS safety significant events are done through\n        the Licensee Event Reports which are sent to CNSNS in accordance with the\n        regulatory provisions.\n\n        Also, LVNPS shares its operational experience with the international nuclear\n        community through the World Association of Nuclear Operators (WANO), which\n        publishes the most significant operational events.\n\n        Regulatory body\n\n        CNSNS participates in the International System for Notification of Events meetings,\n        based on the International Nuclear and Radiological Event Scale (INES) and the\n        International Reporting System for Operating Experience (IRS) meetings, which are\n        jointly held by the IAEA and the Nuclear Energy Agency (NEA)/ Organization for\n        Economic Co-Operation and Development’s (OECD). The objective is to notify and\n        share the experience of the events that occurred during the operation of the nuclear\n        facilities and radiological facilities of Mexico, with the member countries of the\n        international community; as well as take the lessons learned from the member\n        countries. If applicable this experience and lesson learned, they are implemented in\n        Mexican facilities and thus prevent such events from occurring in our country.\n\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-29\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n   -    Policy for use of the INES scale\n\n        Mexico, like all member countries of the INES reportability system, established the\n        policy of informing the IAEA notification system of all radiological events that fall\n        within the scale and of all nuclear events classified as level 2 or higher.\n\n   -    Regulatory review and control activities\n\n        CNSNS verifies the Event Reports presented by CFE, are documented in compliance\n        with the current regulations established in the regulatory framework. When a safety\n        significant event or safety special interest event occurs at LVNPS, CNSNS performs an\n        in-depth assessment and investigation of the causes that originated the event, verifies\n        the existence of recurrent events and based on the initial evaluation of reportable\n        events in accordance with internal procedures, determines the feasibility of carrying\n        out a Special Inspection, in order to verify the actions carried out by LVNPS during the\n        event.\n\n        For the analysis of the events, CNSNS prepares statistics to determine the trends on\n        the type of event, the root cause, the recurrences, and the areas involved in the events,\n        among others, in order to define whether arises the need to apply some regulatory\n        action.\n\n        In addition to the above, the CNSNS has the authority to question or impose the scope\n        of the corrective actions, and/or their deadline for completion, when deemed\n        necessary.\n\n        As mentioned in Article 14, in 2009 CNSNS started categorizing the operational events\n        according to their safety significance. This exercise has the purpose of determining\n        performance indicators similar to USNRC Reactor Oversight Process (ROP)\n        Performance Indicators to compare LVNPS performance with similar plants and\n        emulate the best international practices.\n\nArticle 19 (7)        Operational experience feedback\n\n   -    Overview of the Contracting Party’s arrangements and regulatory\n        requirements on the licence holders to collect and analyse and share\n        operating experience\n\n        The authorisation for commercial operation states that LVNPS shall continue with the\n        feedback from the national and international operational experience and implement\n        changes to relevant legislation that CNSNS considers applicable and within the\n        deadlines established according to the size of the changes, to increase safety and,\n        public and environmental protection.\n\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Their continued operation will generate more spent\n        fuel than originally planned, requiring that spent fuel continue to be stored in the\n        spent fuel pools (SFP).\n\n        Because the SFP were not designed with sufficient capacity to store the spent fuel\n        that will be generated during the plant’s useful life, now considering the Extended\n        Power Uprate (EPU) modification and the Renewal of Operating Authorisation for\n        both units, the need to augment the facilities capacity for spent fuel storage until it is\n        moved to a permanent disposal site has emerged. Therefore, in 2015-2016 an\n        Independent Spent Fuel Storage Installation (ISFSI) was built at LVNPS site.\n\n        The purpose of building this facility is to provide LVNPS with the installed capacity of\n        spent fuel storage, and to recover the storage capacity of both LVNPS units. Then, it\n        will be carried out “campaigns” in which spent fuel assemblies of the SFP of both units\n        are removed and confined in dry storage systems.\n\n        LVNPS successfully completed its Pre-Operational Testing and, as the cut-off date of\n        this National Report, has already hosted five dry storage casks at the ISFSI, containing\n        445 spent fuel assemblies. The facility's capacity will allow the storage of up to 130\n        storage systems, which would translate into 11,570 spent fuel assemblies, thus\n        allowing the plant to operate for up to 60 years.\n\n   -    Implementation of on-site treatment, conditioning and storage of\n        radioactive waste\n\n        Liquid waste treatment\n\n        The liquid waste collected comes from different sources, so they are divided as follows:\n        equipment drains, floor drains, regeneration and chemical drains, detergents and\n        laundry drains, and miscellaneous drains.\n\n        The treatment process is carried out in batches, maintaining surveillance and control\n        of the chemical quality through analysis and sampling. The specific actions depend\n        upon the two following activities:\n\n        a)     For recycling, LVNPS procedures set the chemical parameters that need to be\n               complied with.\n        b)     For release to the environment, the radioactive material is analysed to ensure\n               that any discharge is As Low As Reasonably Achievable (ALARA) and in\n               conformance with the requirements specified in 10 CFR 20 and 10 CFR 50,\n               including the dose design objective specified in 10 CFR 50 Appendix I.\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-36\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        Likewise, with the aim of continuous improvement, in 2008 the \"Reverse Osmosis\"\n        system is put into operation to improve radioactive waste processes. The filtering\n        system, with the use of backwash flow filters (BFF), coal beds, ion exchange resins,\n        Ultra Filtration (UF) and two stages of reverse osmosis, allows LVNPS to treat all high\n        conductivity liquid wastes and high activity that are collected in the Radioactive and\n        Purification Waste Buildings. The above with the purpose of obtaining water with\n        chemical quality that allows it to be re-used in the water-steam cycle.\n\n        Additionally, in the years 2017-2018, the installation of a new Alternate System for the\n        Treatment of High Conductivity and Activity Liquid Waste (ATREX / G60) or High\n        Conductivity Waste Processing (HCWP) System was carried out in the Radioactive\n        Waste Building, Elevation 25.10, with the objective of providing greater processing\n        efficiency of high conductivity and activity wastes. The system has the capacity to\n        process the contributions to the Chemical Waste Concentrator (CWC) System in order\n        to achieve effluents with suitable chemical characteristics to be processed through\n        the Radioactive Floor Drain System (FDR) for liquid radioactive waste, of the Reverse\n        Osmosis System, radioactive distillate filters (MWR) or be discharged into the sea\n        when the chemical and radiological characteristics allow it.\n\n        Gaseous waste treatment\n\n        The gaseous treatment systems are designed according to the origin and radiation\n        levels expected in the following LVNPS extraction and ventilating systems:\n        Radioactive Gaseous (Off-Gas) Waste Treatment System, the Turbine Gland Seal\n        System and Ventilation Systems of different process buildings of the installation.\n\n        It has been verified that the off-gas treatment system is designed to collect and delay\n        exhaust of noble gases produced by fission, which are removed from the condenser\n        by means of steam jet ejectors. The noble gas process continues through a heater, a\n        hydrogen recombiner, a condenser, two 10-minute retention tanks, a pre-filter system,\n        activated carbon beds and high-efficiency air filters, and finally, through a ventilating\n        air current, which is monitored before being sent to the atmosphere.\n\n        This hydrogen recombiner system is designed to support postulated hydrogen\n        (generated by radiolysis) explosions. It has alarms that actuate if a high concentration\n        is detected, which alert the Main Control Room operator to isolate the faulty\n        recombiner and put the redundant train into service.\n\n        Environmental dosimetry studies performed considering LVNPS in operation have\n        demonstrated that design basis assumptions of gas treatment systems are adequate.\n        Based on the above, it is considered that gas treatment satisfies its function of limiting\n        the release of radioactive material in a gaseous form.\n\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-37\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        Solid waste treatment\n\n        This subsystem is designed to collect and process the humid and dry solid waste\n        generated at LVNPS to confine them safely and reliable inside containers appropriate\n        for its eventual isolation. These can be stored in steel containers or high-density\n        polyethylene high-integrity containers (HICs).\n\n        The solid waste processing is controlled by the guidelines of the LVNPS Process\n        Control Programme (PCP), which complies with the Technical Specifications,\n        according to 10 CFR 61 and compliant with the standard NOM-019-NUCL-1995 (see\n        Annexe 7.3 of this National Report).\n\n        The wet solid wastes are treated by the cyclical draining process inside a high-\n        integrity container where the free water content is reduced to less than 1% by volume.\n\n        The dry solid wastes can be treated by one of the following processes:\n\n        a.     For the compactable waste, hydraulic compressing with a 4:1 compacting ratio.\n        b.     For non-compactable waste, direct packaging.\n\n        The sources of wet solid waste are: spent ion exchange resins, sludge of the phase\n        separators, concentrated chemical laboratory waste, regeneration and\n        decontamination solutions. The sources of dry solid waste include: cartridge filters,\n        clothing, paper, plastics, thermal insulation, sand, grit and contaminated replacement\n        components.\n\n        The drums and high-integrity containers with high-level radioactive waste already\n        processed coming from both LVNPS units are initially stored in the decay area, within\n        the Radioactive Waste Building. Later the drums and containers are transferred to the\n        Temporary On-site Storage, which is located in the facility site.\n\n        The polyethylene or steel-shell polyethylene HICs and the steel drums are\n        manufactured with radiation resistance, structural strength, and corrosion resistance,\n        and are consistent with the requirements for seismic design and quality assurance.\n        LVNPS has three areas for the temporary storage of radioactive solid waste:\n\n        1.     In-plant temporary storage (ATP, abbreviation in Spanish). It is inside the\n               Radioactive Waste Building, with a storage capacity for HICs and steel drums of\n               55 gallons, for wet and dry solid waste for two years of waste generation.\n        2.     On-site Temporary Storage (ATS, abbreviation in Spanish). It is located outside\n               the double fence area but on-site. This storage has two warehouses; Warehouse\n               I stores HICs and steel drums of 55 gallons for wet solid waste and is completely\n               full. Warehouse II stores only HICs from the operation of both LVNPS units.\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-38\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        3.     Dry Solid Radioactive Waste Repository (DDRSS, abbreviation in Spanish). It is\n               outside the double fence but on-site, to store steel drums of 55 gallons and\n               metallic containers B25, B12 or special shape for dry solid waste.\n\n        During LVNPS Unit 1 start-up, it was decided to design and build an On-site\n        Temporary Storage installation for wet low and medium radioactive waste; since there\n        was no final repository site. This On-site Temporary Storage was authorized as an\n        extension of the storage area of the Radioactive Waste Treatment Building. This\n        installation was constructed in 1991, with an initial storage capacity for 5 years of\n        operation of LVNPS in both units, considering the original design output of waste.\n        Based on the LVNPS policy for waste reduction, the waste treatment process was\n        improved; and the storage area was restructured, so its capacity was optimised,\n        resulting in an increased storage capacity to accommodate waste up to 2007. The\n        LVNPS wet solid waste storage installed capacity has increased with the construction\n        of the industrial Warehouse II at the On-site Temporary Storage, which is currently in\n        operation.\n\n        Also, a “Dry Solid Radioactive Waste Repository” was enabled and CNSNS granted the\n        authorisation in 1993 to have sufficient storage capacity for dry radioactive waste,\n        which provided an optimised capacity through 2015, assuming a production similar\n        to current volumes. In 2006, studies for the implementation of additional volume\n        reduction methods were started to increase the storage capacity; since the storage is\n        about to be full. In 2007, a super-compaction process was carried out, which reduced\n        the volume by a factor of approximately 3:1 of 6,000 compactable solid waste drums.\n\n        As part of the continuous improvement effort, CFE installed a subsystem of the\n        reverse osmosis system, which consists of a dryer and granulator (currently in\n        standby) that is available to serve both LVNPS units and replaces the evaporators-\n        extruders that have operated for 15 years (which nowadays are inoperable and out of\n        service). In addition, the subsystem technology reduces the final waste compacting\n        ratio to 4:1. With these actions, CFE intends to maintain a reliable waste solidification\n        and reduce the wet solid waste volume. The purpose of this subsystem is to treat the\n        concentrated liquid waste generated during the different stages of the filtering\n        process and the concentrates produced by the waste evaporator system, in order to\n        obtain a dry solid waste with a moisture of less than 1 %. The dry solid waste will be\n        packed in 55-gallon drums for its final disposal.\n\n        Spent Fuel Storage\n\n        The ISFSI is designed to store spent fuel from the commercial operation of LVNPS\n        Units 1 and 2, taking into account the life extension resulting from the Renewal of\n        Operating Authorisation of 30 years. Its purpose is to store the spent fuel assemblies\n        in a safe and reliable manner in suitable containers, which subsequently can be used\n        during transportation to the final disposal site. The HI-STORM FW container system\n        consists of a multi-purpose container (MPC) within an outer casing made of steel and\n        concrete. The outer casing has penetrations in the bottom to allow air circulation to\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-39\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        cool the MPC, and therefore, the spent fuel contained within it. In addition, the MPC\n        has a thermal siphon capacity that allows cooling of the fuel assemblies through the\n        flow of helium in the container. The MPC consists of a basket that holds the fuel\n        assemblies. The basket is made of neutron-absorbing material called Metamic-HT,\n        with properties that ensure control of temperature, reactivity, and structural\n        performance.\n\n        The spent fuel storage complies with the technical specifications for the ISFSI, 10 CFR\n        72, and the standards NOM-004-NUCL-1994 and NOM-035-NUCL-2000.\n\n   -    Activities to keep the amount of waste generated to the minimum\n        practicable for the process concerned, in terms of both activity and volume\n\n        In relation to LVNPS radioactive waste generation minimising processes, several\n        strategies are being put in place by CFE, including:\n\n        1.     Separation of radioactive material and more efficient, cleaner and expeditious\n               decontamination of tools and objects contaminated, in new separation and\n               decontamination workshops.\n        2.     For compactable solid radioactive waste, super-compacting and fragmenting;\n               for not compactable waste, selective decontamination mainly of metallic waste.\n        3.     Optimising the use of materials to maintain equipment, tools and items free of\n               contamination.\n\n\n   -    Established procedures for clearance of radioactive waste\n\n        CFE has a Technical Manual for the Process Control Programme, which is mandatory\n        as prescribed by Technical Specification 3/4.11.3. The manual objective is to ensure that\n        the treatment and conditioning processes are carried out for all the different types of\n        low-level solid radioactive waste generated at LVNPS, such as: spent resins, temporary\n        resins, precoat sludge, sludge from cleaning, sludge concentrates, activated carbon,\n        active dry and other wastes.\n\n   -    Regulatory review and control activities\n\n        As mentioned in Article 14, CNSNS verifies annually the Radioactive Waste\n        Management through its Baseline Inspection Programme. In particular, the\n        inspection verifies the radioactive waste confinement, stability and control at LVNPS\n        installations dedicated to these activities.\n\n        Additionally, during the 2019-2021 period, relating to radioactive waste management,\n        the activities presented below were carried out.\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-40\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        LVNPS storages for radioactive contaminated materials (AMAC)\n\n        Due to the implementation of the Extended Power Uprate (EPU) project in both\n        LVNPS units, in 2007 the Federal Electricity Commission (CFE) initiated the\n        procedures for the construction authorisation of the Storages for Radioactive\n        Contaminated Materials (SRCMs, named AMAC in Spanish) for the temporary storage\n        of dry solid radiologically contaminated materials that resulted from the\n        implementation of the aforementioned project. Of the five AMAC storages proposed,\n        only two were built and authorized by the CNSNS.\n\n        Subsequently, CFE requested CNSNS that the SRCMs could also temporarily store dry\n        solid materials, radiologically contaminated free of transferable contamination, which\n        were generated in LVNPS during its normal operation, refuelling outages and special\n        projects.\n\n        In December 2017, CFE requested CNSNS to extend the operation validity of SRCM\n        storages. Since the authorisation for the operation of these storages would expire on\n        December 31, 2018, in addition, the CFE requested in 2018 that CNSNS authorize the\n        storage of residues with dry solid radioactive material in the SRCMs.\n\n        Finally, in November 2020, the Authorisation to extend the Operating Term of the\n        Storages for Radioactive Contaminated Materials, SRCM 1 and 2, of the LVNPS was\n        granted, as well as an amendment to said authorisation to include the storage of\n        waste with dry solid radioactive material.\n\n        LVNPS storage for radioactive contaminated materials called “4 Naves”\n\n        CFE informed CNSNS that had the necessity for sufficient space for temporary and\n        safe storing of metal containers type B12, B25, ISO and other special forms for the\n        temporary storage of radioactively contaminated dry solid materials, free from\n        transferable contamination, which were generated at LVNPS during normal\n        operation, refuelling outages, and special projects. In the year 2010, CFE requested\n        CNSNS an authorisation for the reconditioning and operation of one LVNPS Storage\n        for Radioactive Contaminated Materials called \"4 Naves\" as a warehouse for this type\n        of containers. The operating authorisation granted for the \"4 Naves\" warehouse\n        expired on December 31, 2018.\n\n        The CFE had planned to carry out the management of the materials stored there\n        during the years 2019 and 2020, so at the end of 2018 requested the CNSNS to grant\n        the authorisation to cease of operations for this warehouse. Additionally, the CFE\n        requested the CNSNS authorisation to store dry solid radioactive material. This\n        request was subsequently cancelled by the CFE in January 2021. In September 2021\n        the CFE informed that the management of the materials stored in the \"4 Naves\"\n        warehouse will conclude in December 2022.\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. Tel: 50006000   www.gob.mx/sener             Period 2019-2021\n                                                           19-41\n\nUNITED MEXICAN STATES\n                                                                                NATIONAL REPORT\n\n\n\n        Finally, in November 2021, the Authorisation to Cease of Operations of the \"4 Naves\"\n        Radiologically Contaminated Materials Warehouse was granted.\n\n        Radioactive Waste Storage ADYR\n\n        In October 2021, the CFE requested authorisation from the CNSNS for the siting stage\n        of the Radioactive Waste Storage Facilities (ADYR). The evaluation of this request\n        began in 2022.\n\n        On-site Independent Spent Fuel Storage Installation (ISFSI) at LVNPS\n\n        In July 2015, CNSNS, anticipating CFE’s application, issued the construction\n        authorisation for the facility to be located on the LVNPS property. This authorisation\n        was supported by an evaluation of the respective safety analysis report and a focused\n        inspection of the construction process.\n\n        Also during 2014 and 2015, CNSNS evaluated the Safety Analysis Report, putting into\n        consideration three private international companies that competed with CFE for its\n        fuel assemblies storage cask technology to be adopted for the ISFSI.\n\n        Subsequently, the CFE submitted to the CNSNS the request to operate this facility.\n        This request was composed of the Final Safety Analysis Report, Quality Assurance\n        Program, Environmental Report, Technical Specifications (TS) and Report 10 CFR\n        72.212. The regulatory evaluation covered the items of evaluation, inspection, physical\n        security and safeguards, and generated 119 Requests for Additional Information. The\n        CNSNS also witnessed the Pre-Operational Tests: Welding, Drying and Filling,\n        Container Loading and Transfer to the ISFSI.\n\n        Currently, the CFE has the Operating Authorisation issued by CNSNS in June 2019 with\n        a 40-year validity and two transfers, called \"campaigns\", of fuel containers from the\n        LVNPS Buildings to the ISFSI warehouse have already been carried out.\n\n\n\n\nAvenida de los Insurgentes Sur #890, Col del Valle\nAlcaldía Benito Juárez, CP. 03100 CDMX. "29.06.2023 - 23:58\nCompartir por WhatsApp\nCompartir por Twitter\nCompartir por Facebook\nCompartir por Linkedin\nLa Secretaría de la Defensa Nacional (Sedena) sufrió un total de 6,124 intentos de ciberataque durante el periodo comprendido del 1 de noviembre del 2022 al 10 de abril del presente año, es decir, 38 ataques diarios.\nDe acuerdo con la solicitud de información con número de folio 330026423001306, estos eventos se registraron después del robo de información más grande de la historia a la dependencia federal que estuvo a cargo del grupo autodenominado como Guacamaya.\nTras este evento, se le cuestionó a la dependencia federal qué medidas habría tomado para impedir un nuevo ciberataque de la magnitud del que se registró en el 2022, ante esto la Sedena respondió:\n“Se hace de su conocimiento que después del evento de ciberseguridad, se han implementado: actualizaciones de software, buenas prácticas de ciberseguridad recomendadas a nivel internacional, fortalecimiento de las medidas de ciberseguridad y la capacitación al personal”.\nInvestigación\nEn dicha respuesta, la Unidad de Asuntos Jurídicos de la Secretaría de la Defensa también confirmó que no se ha interpuesto ninguna denuncia ante la Fiscalía General del República con motivo de esta filtración de documentos por parte del grupo hacktivistas Guacamaya.\nSin embargo, a la pregunta específica sobre si existe una investigación interna en la Secretaría de la Defensa Nacional por ese ciberataque de Guacamaya y, de ser así, a cuántos elementos militares se ha sancionado, la información fue declarada por el Ejército como reservada.\n“La Dirección General de Informática y la Fiscalía General de Justicia Militar hace de su conocimiento que se encuentra con impedimento legal para proporcionar la información que solicita en observancia el artículo 215 del código militar de procedimientos penales donde se establece que los registros de la investigación incluyendo documentos fotografías videos grabaciones y cualquier otro medio de prueba son estrictamente reservados por lo que su acceso únicamente le compete a las partes”, refirió la respuesta.\nSin embargo, a finales de marzo, se dio a conocer la detención de un mando militar de informática quien fue recluido en la prisión del Campo Militar 1-A en la Ciudad de México.\nEl militar fue acusado de infracción a los deberes militares, en la modalidad de pérdida de información militar por la Fiscalía de Justicia Militar en torno a la extracción de miles de documentos clasificados de la dependencia.\nNo obstante, el 5 de abril de este año, un juez militar concedió libertad provisional al imputado ya que consideró que el delito por el que se le acuso no amerita prisión preventiva oficiosa.\nEntre los temas relevantes que revelaron los millones de correos de la Sedena filtrados, se encuentra que el área de inteligencia del Ejército mantendría investigaciones sobre los gobiernos de algunos estados, entre los que está Morelos, Sin embargo, no ha sido la única vulneración de gran calado en la Administración de Andrés Manuel López Obrador, ni tampoco existen garantías de que un evento semejante no se repita. La Auditoría Superior de la Federación ha publicado informes en los que ha dado cuenta de carencias y fallas en los sistemas de ciberseguridad de varias instituciones que no han sido atendidas. Los diagnósticos corresponden en su mayoría al periodo del Gobierno de López Obrador, pues en sexenios anteriores no se elaboraban auditorías sobre la gestión de las tecnologías de información institucionales y la administración de riesgos.\nEl ciberataque más reciente afectó los servidores de la Secretaría de Infraestructura, Comunicaciones y Transportes (SICT) el pasado 24 de octubre. La dependencia publicó que un\nsoftware\nmalicioso infectó equipos de cómputo, lo que obligó a suspender sus sistemas para evitar vulneraciones graves y un posible robo de información. Como consecuencia de la infección, la SICT\nsuspendió los trámites\nde licencias y certificados del sector aéreo y autotransporte. La intrusión también provocó un retraso en los adiestramientos e incrementos salariales de los pilotos aviadores, de acuerdo con\nReforma\n.\nLa Auditoría Superior, el órgano encargado de comprobar cómo se gasta el dinero del erario asignado a las instituciones públicas, detectó desde 2019 que la SICT presentaba vulnerabilidades en materia de seguridad informática. “Se aplicó con deficiencias la metodología para el desarrollo de soluciones tecnológicas, lo cual propicia imperfecciones en la gestión de riesgos, el análisis de vulnerabilidades previo a la puesta en marcha de los aplicativos, las pruebas técnicas y funcionales, así como en el aseguramiento de la calidad de los sistemas”, señala la auditoría 346-DE.\nEl hackeo a la Secretaría de la Defensa Nacional (Sedena) puso al descubierto información delicada sobre el estado de salud del presidente Andrés Manuel López Obrador;\nreportes de inteligencia\nsobre cárteles de la droga; diagnósticos sobre la penetración de grupos delincuenciales en la capital de México y otras ciudades, y negociaciones bilaterales sobre\nextradiciones de narcotraficantes\na Estados Unidos. También reveló datos sobre violaciones a los derechos humanos cometidas dentro y por el Ejército, como el uso de\nprogramas de espionaje\npara vigilar a objetivos no criminales, como periodistas, activistas y funcionarios; el registro de denuncias de\nabusos sexuales\nno atendidas; o cifras del elevado\níndice de letalidad\n\nreportado en operativos castrenses.\nEn 2021, un año antes del\nhackeo de Guacamaya\n, la Auditoría señaló que la dependencia no había implementado medidas para identificar brechas de seguridad ni había corregido vulnerabilidades. Tampoco había llevado a cabo configuraciones necesarias en servicios de internet, correo electrónico y dispositivos extraíbles para limitar el tipo de información que podía ingresar o salir de la secretaría y para evitar la instalación de\nsoftware\nmalicioso en sus servidores y estaciones de trabajo. La Sedena tampoco implementó medidas básicas como supervisar que sus proveedores de tecnología efectuaran mantenimientos preventivos, instalaran actualizaciones o capacitaran al personal de la institución. Todas estas fallas, sostuvo la Auditoría, representaban un riesgo a la integridad, confidencialidad y disponibilidad de los sistemas de información, lo que ponía en riesgo, desde entonces, la operación de la Sedena, de acuerdo con el informe 68-GB.\nEn 2021, el grupo de ciberdelincuentes Avaddon\nsecuestró información de la Lotería Nacional\ne hizo pública una parte tras no haber obtenido el pago de un rescate de parte del Gobierno mexicano. El grupo de\nransomware\ndio a conocer que tenía en su poder documentos legales y financieros, contratos e información sobre un caso de acoso sexual dentro de la empresa paraestatal. La Auditoría Superior determinó que el hackeo se debió a que los servidores funcionaban con versiones del sistema operativo sin actualizaciones de seguridad. “Los servidores afectados por el ataque cibernético tenían versiones de sistemas operativos fuera del soporte del fabricante; además, ninguno contaba con soluciones para la prevención de pérdida de datos ni con las actualizaciones de seguridad (parches); asimismo, se identificaron deficiencias en la supervisión periódica de las actividades de las cuentas privilegiadas para detectar infracciones a las políticas de seguridad, así como transacciones irregulares en los servidores de misión crítica”, señaló el órgano fiscalizador en la auditoría 187.\nOtro ciberataque a gran escala afectó a la paraestatal Petróleos Mexicanos (Pemex) en noviembre de 2019, el primer año del Gobierno de López Obrador. Un grupo de hackers tomó el control de unos 6.000 equipos de los que se extrajeron 180 mil archivos. El ataque afectó la operación de las refinerías de Veracruz y Tabasco. La Auditoría determinó que en ese hackeo hubo responsabilidad de Pemex, más que del fabricante del sistema operativo (Microsoft), pues este corrigió la vulnerabilidad de los servidores expuestos seis meses antes del ataque. “Debido a la falta de gestión de actualizaciones de seguridad (parches), entre otros controles, la vulnerabilidad no fue remediada por Pemex, lo que contribuyó para que los equipos de cómputo hayan sido secuestrados, ocasionando la pérdida de activos de información en los servidores y equipos de usuario final, así como la interrupción de los procesos de negocio de la empresa”, se indicó en el informe 413-DE. El auditor detectó, además, que la paraestatal tenía equipos de cómputo con sistemas operativos obsoletos y que no contaban con soporte de parte del fabricante, “lo cual aumenta el riesgo para la seguridad de la información, debido a la falta de actualizaciones de seguridad para remediar vulnerabilidades que están expuestas a ataques cibernéticos”.\nSuscríbase aquí\na la\nnewsletter\nde EL PAÍS México y reciba todas las claves informativas de la actualidad de este país\nSobre la firma\nZedryk Raziel\nVer biografía\nReportero de EL PAÍS México, especialista en la cobertura de asuntos políticos y de corrupción. While the government does not generally impose restrictions on anonymity or encryption for internet users, investigative reporting has revealed some ongoing restrictions on users of the Tor network, a censorship circumvention tool.\nAn investigation conducted between 2020 and October 2023 found that the Mexican government blocks 39 URLs from 21 Mexican government agencies for users of the Tor network.\n1\nA representative from the president’s office claimed that the “protection measures,” which have reportedly been in place for at least 12 years, are necessary to block traffic that is “malicious, automated, or that could be a threat.” In response, a Tor representative stated that the government’s actions are a disproportionate and an “isolating” way to safeguard against potential cyber harms.\n2\nData from the Open Observatory of Network Interference (OONI) showed that Tor remained widely accessible in Mexico during the coverage period, indicating that there had not been wider efforts to restrict Tor itself (see B1).\n3\nPreviously, in April 2022,\n4\nthe SCJN struck down the creation of a problematic biometric cell phone registry that had been established through a reform to the Federal Telecommunications and Broadcasting Law passed by Congress in April 2021. The court echoed criticism from digital rights organizations in its ruling, citing the registry’s unnecessary infringement on privacy rights and inadequate data safeguards.\n\n5\nUsers would have been required to join the registry when purchasing a SIM card or activating a new prepaid mobile line.\n6\n1\nJacbo Nájera and Miguel Trujillo, “Gobierno mexicano y la red Tor [Mexican government and the Tor network],” GitHub, October 2023,\nhttps://github.com/tor-gob/reporte-mexico\n.\n2\nJacobo Nájera and Miguel Trujillo, “Mexican government faces criticism for blocking portions of the safe internet,” Global Voices, October 12, 2023,\nhttps://advox.globalvoices.org/2023/10/12/mexican-government-faces-crit…\n.\n3\nOONI, “OONI Measurement Aggregation Toolkit (MAT): Tor Test, Mexico,” OONI Explorer, accessed September 2024,\nhttps://explorer.ooni.org/chart/mat?probe_cc=MX&since=2023-09-16&until=…\n.\n4\nDarinka Rodríguez, “La Suprema Corte de México invalida la creación de un padrón de datos biométricos para usuarios de celulares” [Mexican Supreme Court invalidates the creation of a biometric registry for mobile phone users], El País, April 25, 2022,\nhttps://elpais.com/mexico/2022-04-25/la-suprema-corte-de-mexico-invalid…\n.\n5\n“Mexico’s president can prevent a privacy disaster: veto the new biometric mobile phone registry,” AccessNow, April 16, 2021,\nhttps://www.accessnow.org/mexicos-new-biometric-mobile-phone-registry/\n; Alessandro Mascellino, “Judge blocks Mexico’s biometric cell phone registry requirement,” Biometric Update, April 21, 2021,\nhttps://www.biometricupdate.com/202104/judge-blocks-mexicos-biometric-c…\n.\nC5\n1.00-6.00 pts\n0-6 pts\nDoes state surveillance of internet activities infringe on users’ right to privacy?\n1.00\n1\n6.00\n6\nThe government has used the poor security situation in the country to justify expanding the state’s surveillance powers, with little accountability and oversight. High-profile abuses of digital spying technologies meant for law enforcement purposes continued to emerge during the coverage period, but they have not been thoroughly investigated by authorities.\nIn February 2024, an investigation from R3D detailed the functions of the Cyberspace Operations Center (COC), a covert division of SEDENA created in 2016 to conduct “military operations in cyberspace.”\n1\nAccording to the investigation, the COC conducts social media monitoring of users who criticize the Mexican military or the federal government, at times creating inauthentic user accounts in an attempt to infiltrate trusted networks online and uncover more information about critical users. The investigation found that the COC uses a specialized software created by Israeli company WebintPro, called HIWIRE, to monitor social media activities and identify links between users, as well as to deploy bots to “exert soft influence on public opinion” (see B5).\n2\nThese activities are known through leaked documents;\n3\nthere is no legal basis for the operations of the COC in existing Mexican law and the contract that SEDENA signed to acquire HIWIRE has not been made publicly available.\n\nThe Mexican military is reported to be one of the world’s largest users of Pegasus spyware, which can surveil all activities on mobile devices with no apparent signs of a breach.\n4\nPegasus has been used to target those investigating government corruption and human rights abuses in Mexico.\n5\nA joint investigation by Citizen Lab and the Mexican civil society organization R3D revealed that two human rights defenders, Jorge Santiago Aguirre Espinosa and María Luisa Aguilar Rodríguez, both working at the Miguel Agustín Pro Juárez Human Rights Center (Centro PRODH), were targeted by Pegasus between June and September 2022. According to Citizen Lab, the alleged timing of the surveillance suggests that the military was involved in these cases, though it was not able to confirm this. At the time the spyware was apparently active on Aguirre and Aguilar’s devices, Centro PRODH had been investigating past human rights abuses committed by the Mexican Army and providing support for the relatives of those who were forcibly disappeared by the military.\n6\nIn October 2022, the #EjércitoEspía investigation, coordinated by several civil society organizations and media outlets, documented three additional cases of suspected military espionage using Pegasus. Human rights defender Raymundo Ramos and two journalists, Ricardo Raphael and one from the outlet Animal Político, were reportedly surveilled with Pegasus between 2019 and 2021.\n7\nThe evidence presented in the investigation, which includes forensic analysis by Citizen Lab, confirmed that the victims were targeted with zero-click exploits, which do not require any action from victims and are virtually undetectable.\n8\nAll three individuals had recently denounced human rights abuses by the Mexican armed forces, suggesting that the Mexican military was responsible for the illegal spying.\n9\nIn March 2023, the same organizations that produced the #EjércitoEspía investigation released new evidence about the surveillance of human rights defender Raymundo Ramos.\n10\nInternal documents obtained from SEDENA appeared to conclusively demonstrate that the military used Pegasus to spy on Ramos through the highly secretive Military Intelligence Center (CMI), which lacks any legal basis for spying on civilians. According to the investigation, the CMI operates under the command of military leadership and spied on Ramos in order to disrupt his journalistic investigation into the army’s human rights abuses.\n11\nIn May 2023, the\nNew York Times\nreported that Alejandro Encinas, the country’s undersecretary for human rights and a close ally of President López Obrador, had been targeted with Pegasus spyware. Encinas had recently investigated potential abuses by the military and had been publicly critical of the armed forces.\n12\nIn June, during the coverage period, the\nWashington Post\npublished an investigation reporting that historian Camilo Vicente Ovalle, who coordinates a truth commission about Mexico’s “Dirty War” as part of Encinas’ office, was also targeted with Pegasus in the latter half of 2022;\n13\na development which was condemned by the truth commission’s Historical Clarification Mechanism and by digital rights organizations.\n\n14\nPresident López Obrador denied the evidence presented by the organizations and the media outlets, repeatedly claiming that his government had not used Pegasus to spy on Encinas or other figures.\n15\nAfter the October 2022 #EjércitoEspía investigation was published, López Obrador dismissed claims that the government had spied on journalist Ricardo Raphael because “it would be a waste of time” to do so. He also claimed that the army does not spy, but rather does “intelligence.”\n16\nDespite these revelations, authorities have been slow to investigate, and past cases have continued to go unpunished.\n17\nIn January 2023, the INAI ordered SEDENA to carry out a thorough search and provide information related to the contracts that the military signed with the company Comercializadora Antsua, the exclusive supplier of the Pegasus spyware in Mexico.\n18\nAfter SEDENA refused to comply for more than a year, in June 2024, after the coverage period, a court ordered the military to turn over the documents.\n19\nThe government was first implicated in the use of Pegasus when the results of the Pegasus Project investigation were published in July 2021. Leaked data from NSO Group, originally accessed by Forbidden Stories and Amnesty International and shared with 15 media organizations, confirmed and expanded upon prior reporting on the Mexican government’s use of Pegasus against critical voices.\n20\nSome 15,000 phone numbers from a leaked list of 50,000—which are presumed to belong to the targets of NSO clients—belonged to people located in Mexico, including journalists and politicians; the Mexican bloc was the largest in the entire list.\n21\nIn November 2021, the Attorney General’s Office (FGR) announced its first detention in the Pegasus investigation: Juan Carlos García Rivera, an employee in one of the dozen private companies that served as intermediaries between the Mexican government and NSO Group. García Rivera was accused of spying on journalist Carmen Aristegui via Pegasus in a way that “affected, limited, and undermined her freedom of expression.”\n22\nIn January 2024, a judge in Mexico City acquitted García Rivera, determining that Aristegui had been spied on with Pegasus for her journalistic work but that prosecutors had failed to prove that García Rivera was directly responsible.\n23\nIn response, Article 19, R3D, and SocialTIC emphasized that actions seeking to ensure accountability must continue.\n24\nOfficials have access to other surveillance tools with interception capabilities. In April 2023, Citizen Lab reported that Israeli spyware vendor QuaDream had been contracted by several government clients, including Mexico, to provide surveillance technology.\n25\nLike Pegasus, QuaDream is reportedly a zero-click exploit. Previously, in May 2020, three civil society organizations found 21 active international mobile subscriber identity (IMSI)–catchers, which can be used to intercept mobile data from all devices in the immediate area, in central Mexico.\n26", "excerpt_sha256": "2a3b27809ff6bbee6612d5a377b50a2bc46d1164e90b0a4ad43240edc6312d18", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0018-E003", "source_id": "SRC-5a5b157ea97d69d8", "url": "https://articulo19.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/03/Voces-contra-la-Indiferencia-INF-A19-22-PDF-Prel-v2.pdf", "title": "Voces contra la indiferencia: informe anual 2022", "publisher": "ARTICLE 19 Oficina para México y Centroamérica", "raw_sha256": "2b0e7e8b086e96c361242587d9cfdbcdc7ae92deb99eaaff9157be105948296b", "fact_summary": "ARTICLE 19 reports Pegasus espionage against a human-rights defender and two journalists during the current administration, including military surveillance; it states that the president denied the reports and that official resources were used for a campaign to contest them.
